The biceps line is the HARD floor: a sleeve narrower than the biceps\n59\t    // girth + ease binds or won't close at the underarm. Classic set-in drafting\n60\t    // (Aldrich/Brian) draws the biceps line first, then trues the cap to the\n61\t    // armhole by adjusting the cap HEIGHT — not the width.\n62\t    //\n63\t    // Step 1: solve width by length-matching at the default cap height. Cap\n64\t    // length grows monotonically with width, so bisect (a multiplicative walk\n65\t    // oscillates and can miss the tolerance).\n66\t    double lo = 60.0;\n67\t    double hi = std::max(bicepsEstimate, 200.0) * 3;\n68\t    while (capCurveLength(hi, capHeight) < targetCapLength && hi < 8000) {\n69\t        hi *= 1.5;\n70\t    }\n71\t    double width = std::max(bicepsEstimate, 200.0);\n72\t    for (int i = 0; i < 60; ++i) {\n73\t        width = (lo + hi) / 2;\n74\t        const double delta = capCurveLength(width, capHeight) - targetCapLength;\n75\t        if (std::fabs(delta) <= convergenceTolerance) break;\n76\t        if (delta > 0) hi = width; else lo = width;\n77\t    }\n78\t\n79\t    // Step 2: if that width is below the biceps floor (the common case — a\n80\t    // length-matched cap on the default height comes out too narrow for the\n81\t    // arm), widen to the biceps and instead lower the cap height until the cap\n82\t    // length matches again. A wider sleeve on a shallower cap is the correct,\n83\t    // sewable relaxed-set trade; the cap ease stays inside the validator window.\n84\t    if (width < bicepsEstimate) {\n85\t        width = bicepsEstimate;\n86\t        double chLo = 20.0, chHi = capHeight; // shallower cap => shorter cap length\n87\t        // At width=biceps the default height overshoots the target, so the\n88\t        // answer sits below capHeight; bisect the height down to the target.\n89\t        for (int i = 0; i < 60; ++i) {\n90\t            capHeight = (chLo + chHi) / 2;\n91\t            const double delta = capCurveLength(width, capHeight) - targetCapLength;\n92\t            if (std::fabs(delta) <= convergenceTolerance) break;\n93\t            if (delta > 0) chHi = capHeight; else chLo = capHeight;\n94\t        }\n95\t    }\n96\t\n97\t    // GATHERED / PUFF HEAD (Loop 6). Slash-and-spread across the crown: the cap\n98\t    // is widened by `spread` and the top point is drawn HIGHER so the extra arc\n99\t    // is gathered/eased into the SAME armhole (crown gather between the notches;\n100\t    // the underarm-to-notch length still matches the armhole 1:1). VERIFIED rule\n101\t    // (dresspatternmaking / M.Müller): a puff RAISES the cap by the spread amount;\n102\t    // a plain gathered head keeps the height. The biceps/hem width below is left\n103\t    // on the fitted base `width` so the sleeve still clears the arm. See FORMULAS.\n104\t    const double spread = capSpreadFrac(cap) * width;      // 0 when Plain / Cap\n105\t    const double capWidth = width + spread;                // widened crown\n106\t    const double capRise = (cap == SleeveCap::Puffed) ? spread : 0.0;\n107\t    capHeight += capRise;                                  // Plain/Cap: +0 → identical\n108\t\n109\t    // CAP SLEEVE WING (R1.2). A cap sleeve keeps the ordinary set-in cap head\n110\t    // (the same cap curve, fitted to the armhole above so it sets in 1:1) but has\n111\t    // NO underarm seam and NO length — the outer edge sweeps a short depth below\n112\t    // the crown and back up to the underarm points, so a small wing covers the\n113\t    // top of the shoulder and dies at the underarm. We draw it here as its own\n114\t    // outline and return early; the full-sleeve body below is skipped. Only a\n115\t    // Short cap is a cap sleeve — length is ignored (a cap has no length axis).\n116\t    if (cap == SleeveCap::Cap) {\n117\t        const double cHalf = width / 2;         // the fitted cap width (armhole-matched)\n118\t        const Point cl{-cHalf, capHeight};      // left underarm point (cap base)\n119\t        const Point cr{cHalf, capHeight};       // right underarm point\n120\t        const Point apex{0, 0};                 // cap crown\n121\t        // The wing hangs capWingDepth below the crown at the shoulder point and\n122\t        // tapers to nothing at the underarm points, so the outer edge is a shallow\n123\t        // arc from cl down past (0, capHeight + capWingDepth) back to cr.\n124\t        const double wingY = capHeight + capWingDepth;\n125\t        std::vector<PathCommand> capCmds{PathCommand::move(cl)};\n126\t        // cap head (armhole edge): left underarm → crown → right underarm, the\n127\t        // SAME S-curve the plain cap uses, so it eases into the armhole identically.\n128\t        for (const auto& c : capCurve(cl, apex, true)) capCmds.push_back(c);\n129\t        for (const auto& c : capCurve(apex, cr, false)) cap\n132\t        // base so there is no seam to sew shut).\n133\t        capCmds.push_back(PathCommand::curve(\n134\t            {0, wingY}, {cHalf * 0.7, capHeight + capWingDepth * 0.55}, {cHalf * capCmds.push_back(PathCommand::close());\n138\t\n139\t        // Notches on the cap so it matches the armhole notches when set in.\n140\t        std::vector<PathCommand> capMarks{\n141\t {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018ZQcbPxd9nXMeGLknBgqhN","type":"tool_result","content":"// binding measure can never drift from the drawn geometry, and the princess\n// split (splitCubic) can keep assuming a single cubic.\n//\n// This is a proper scye, not a lazy diagonal (the old control points produced a\n// near-straight bulge). The curve leaves the shoulder tip TANGENT-CONTINUOUS\n// with the shoulder seam (no angular V-kink at the shoulder point — the old\n// cp1 dived straight down while the shoulder seam ran out nearly horizontal, a\n// ~77 degree corner the external render-audit flagged as a \"spike\"), rolls\n// smoothly downward, hollows INWARD in the middle (the concave scye), and\n// arrives at the underarm near-tangent to the side seam so the underarm is a\n// smooth turn. The FRONT scye is scooped deeper than the BACK (anatomy).\n// Rendered as a native SVG cubic, so it is perfectly smooth; the control points\n// carry both the tangent continuity and the scye hollow.\n//   neckPoint — the shoulder-neck point; shoulderTip->this direction reversed is\n//               the shoulder seam's incoming tangent, which the curve leaves along.\n//   isFront   — deeper hollow on the front piece.\n//   sleeveless — cut the shoulder tip in and raise the underarm so a bare\n//                shoulder edge sits close to the body instead of gaping.\nPathCommand armholeCurveFor(double shoulderHalf, double shoulderDrop,\n                            const Point& armholeBottomIn, const Point& neckPoint,\n                            bool isFront, bool sleeveless, bool setIn = false) {\n    Point shoulder{shoulderHalf, shoulderDrop};\n    Point armholeBottom = armholeBottomIn;\n    if (sleeveless) {\n        // Cut the armhole in: pull the shoulder tip toward the neck and raise the\n        // underarm point, so the finished (bias-bound) edge hugs the body.\n        shoulder.x -= BodiceBlock::sleevelessShoulderCutInMM;\n        armholeBottom.y -= BodiceBlock::sleevelessUnderarmRaiseMM;\n    }\n    const double dx = armholeBottom.x - shoulder.x;   // horizontal span (>0)\n    const double dy = armholeBottom.y - shoulder.y;   // vertical drop   (>0)\n    if (setIn) {\n        // Set-in-sleeve scye (opt-in): cp1 breaks from the shoulder seam and heads\n        // DOWN into the armhole — a real set-in armhole corners at the tip and the\n        // sleeve cap covers it. Deeper hollow than the sleeveless/tangent scye.\n        // No tangent lock (that lock is what a single cubic could not reconcile\n        // with a set-in scye — the measured 20.6 mm structural residual).\n        const double hollowIn = (isFront ? BodiceBlock::setInArmholeHollowShareFront\n                                         : BodiceBlock::setInArmholeHollowShareBack) * dx;\n        const Point cp1In{\n            shoulder.x + dx * BodiceBlock::setInArmholeCp1OutShare,\n            shoulder.y + dy * BodiceBlock::setInArmholeUpperDropShare};\n        const Point cp2In{\n            armholeBottom.x - dx * 0.06 - hollowIn,\n            shoulder.y + dy * BodiceBlock::setInArmholeLowerDropShare};\n        return PathCommand::curve(armholeBottom, cp1In, cp2In);\n    }\n    const double hollow = (isFront ? BodiceBlock::armholeHollowShareFront\n                                   : BodiceBlock::armholeHollowShareBack) * dx;\n    const double chord = std::hypot(dx, dy);\n\n    // Shoulder-seam tangent AT the tip: the seam runs neckPoint -> shoulderTip,\n    // so its outgoing direction (continuing past the tip) is that unit vector.\n    // The armhole leaves the tip ALONG this direction so the seam and the scye\n    // share a tangent — no corner. cp1 sits a modest reach out along it, then\n    // the hollow pulls it back inward so the curve still dives into the scye.\n    double stx = shoulder.x - neckPoint.x;\n    double sty = shoulder.y - neckPoint.y;\n    const double slen = std::hypot(stx, sty);\n    if (slen > 1e-6) { stx /= slen; sty /= slen; }\n    else { stx = 1.0; sty = 0.0; }\n    // cp1 leaves the tip PURELY along the shoulder-seam tangent — nothing pulls\n    // it sideways, so the seam and the scye share a tangent and the old ~77 deg\n    // spike is gone. The scye hollow is carried entirely by cp2 (mid/lower),\n    // which keeps the belly concave without breaking the shoulder tangent.\n    const double tanReach = chord * BodiceBlock::armholeShoulderTangentShare;\n    const Point cp1{\n        shoulder.x + stx * tanReach,\n        shoulder.y + sty * tanReach};\n    // cp2: near the underarm, pulled deep INSIDE the chord (the concave scye\n    // belly), rising toward the underarm near-vertically so the turn into the\n    // side seam is smooth. All of the hollow lives here now.\n    const Point cp2{\n        armholeBottom.x - dx * 0.06 - hollow,\n        shoulder.y + dy * BodiceBlock::armholeLowerDropShare};\n    return PathCommand::curve(armholeBottom, cp1, cp2);\n}\n\n// y on the waist bezier at a given x (the curve is monotonic in x).\ndouble waistCurveY(double x, Point sideWaist, const PathCommand& curve) {\n    if (curve.type != CmdType::Curve) return sideWaist.y;\n    Point best = sideWaist;\n    double bestDistance = std::numeric_limits<double>::max();","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_012YXWB2qVAqm8SL5t2YD6DS","session_id":"3986d631-caa8-4bb8-aa71-9a5b56421d00","uuid":"2d3c3b56-ab8b-4ea8-90e9-2fb4e5bafab2","timestamp":"2026-08-22T12:56:42.746Z","subagent_type":"isci-motor","task_description":"F6-B kol oyuğu kapak ölçümü"}

ÖNCE BU: BU DEFTER KAYNAKSIZ ÇIKTI — ARAÇ ERİŞİMİ REDDEDİLDİ\n\nBu kartın tek işi \"kaynaklı sayı getirmek\"ti. **Getirilemedi.** Bu oturumda web\naraçlarının ikisi de izinsiz:\n\n| araç | denenen | dönen |\n|---|---|---|\n| `WebSearch` | `sleeve cap ease amount woven set-in sleeve ... standard` | `Claude requested permissions to use WebSearch, but you haven't granted it yet.` |\n| `WebSearch` | `\"sleeve cap ease\" chart by fabric knit woven percentage industry standard` | aynı hata |\n| `WebSearch` (allowed_domains: threadsmagazine.com, clo3d.com, seamly.net) | `sleeve cap ease standard millimeters armhole` | aynı hata |\n| `WebFetch` | `https://www.threadsmagazine.com/2008/10/01/perfect-sleeve-cap-ease` | `Claude requested permissions to use WebFetch, but you haven't granted it yet.` |\n| `WebFetch` | `https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sleeve` | aynı hata |\n\nYani: **tek bir URL çekilmedi, tek bir birincil kaynak okunmadı.**\n\nDEĞİŞMEZ 1 gereği burada duruyorum: erişemediğim kaynağı \"erişilemedi\" diye\nyazıyorum, tahmin etmiyorum. Hepsi toptan **DOĞRULANMADI**.\nBilgi silmek, vermemekten beterdir (DEĞİŞMEZ 8) — o yüzden atılmadı, ama\netiketlendi ve kapıdan uzak tutuldu.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Dokuma set-in kolda cap ease bandı — **DOĞRULANMADI (kaynak çekilemedi)**\n\nHafızadan hatırlanan, **doğrulanmamış** literatür iddiaları:\n\n- **Armstrong, *Patternmaking for Fashion Design*** — dokuma set-in kol için\n  kapak yedirmesi tipik olarak **1\"–1.5\" (≈25–38 mm)** toplam olarak anılır.\n  → **DOĞRULANMADI.** Kitap birincil kaynak ama sayfa/baskı doğrulanmadı, alıntı\n  çekilmedi.\n- **Aldrich, *Metric Pattern Cutting for Women's Wear*** — elbise/bluz kolunda\n  yedirme daha düşük anılır (**≈15–25 mm**), terzilik ceketinde daha yüksek\n  (**≈30–45 mm**). → **DOĞRULANMADI.**\n- **Threads Magazine** yazılarında \"1\"–1.5\" gelenekseldir ama çoğu modern kumaşta\n  **3/8\"–3/4\" (≈10–20 mm)** yeterlidir\" tartışması geçer. → **DOĞRULANMADI**,\n  üstelik dergi = ikincil/atölye kaynağı, kitap ayarında değil.\n- Oran olarak: yaklaşık 400–460 mm'lik bir kol oyuğunda 25–38 mm ≈ **%5.5–%9**.\n  Bu **benim aritmetiğim**, kaynakta oran olarak yazdığını doğrulamadım.\n\n**Mutlak mm mi, oran mı?** Literatürün mm/inç (mutlak) verdiğini, endüstri\ntech-pack'lerinin ise oyuk uzunluğuna oranla düşündüğünü hatırlıyorum; **hangisinin\nstandart olduğu bu turda doğrulanamadı.** Bandın alt/üst ucunu ayrı ayrı veren\ntablolu bir kaynak **bulunamadı, çünkü aranamadı.**\n\n## 2. Kumaşa bağımlılık — **DOĞRULANMADI**\n\n- Örme (knit): yedirme **0'a yakın veya negatif**; örme kumaş kolun kendisi\n  esnediği için kapağa fazlalık koymaya gerek kalmaz. Sayı olarak **0–13 mm\n  (0–1/2\")** anılır. → **DOĞRULANMADI.**\n- Kalın yün / melton / kaba dokuma: yedirmeyi **taşıyabilir** (lif sıkışabilir,\n  ütüyle çekilebilir), bandın üst ucu buraya bakar. → **DOĞRULANMADI.**\n- İpek / sık dokuma / gabardin: yedirme **büzülme kırışığı (pucker)** yapar,\n  bandın alt ucu buraya bakar. → **DOĞRULANMADI.**\n\nKumaş sınıfı ↔ mm veren tablolu bir kaynak (varsa Müller & Sohn ya da bir\nendüstri tech-pack) **çekilemedi.**\n\n## 3. Bedene bağımlılık (grade) — **DOĞRULANMADI, ZAYIF**\n\nBu soruya dürüst cevabım: **bilmiyorum ve doğrulayamadım.** Hafızamda\n\"cap ease bedenle nasıl gradelenir\" konusunda güvenilir bir literatür maddesi\nYOK. İki rakip hipotez var, ikisi de **kaynaksız**:\n\n- (a) mm sabit tutulur (yedirme bir *dikiş tekniği* limitidir, kumaşın\n  cm başına yutabildiği fazlalıkla sınırlıdır → mutlak),\n- (b) oran sabit tutulur (oyuk bedenle büyüdüğü için yedirme de büyür).\n\n**Bu ayrım kapı için kritik** ve tam da kaynak gerektiren yer. Sonraki işçi\nöncelikli olarak bunu aramalı.\n\n## 4. Ease ↔ büzgü (gather / fullness) sınırı — **DOĞRULANMADI**\n\n- \"Yedirilebilir\" fazlalığın üst sınırı olarak **~1.5–2\" (≈38–50 mm)** anılır;\n  bunun üstü kumaşta düz kalmaz, **büzgü ilan edilmek zorundadır.** →\n  **DOĞRULANMADI**, atölye lore'u seviyesinde.\n- Puf kolda eklenen fazlalık **%25–%100** mertebesinde tarif edilir (yani ease\n  değil, açıkça fullness). → **DOĞRULANMADI.**\n- **Slash-and-spread ↔ kapak yüksekliği:** hatırladığım kural, kapağı sadece\n  yatayda yayarsan puf çöker; hacmi ayakta tutmak için kapak **yüksekliğinin de\n  yükseltilmesi** gerekir. Yayılan genişlik ile yükseltme arasında verilen sayısal\n  bir oran (ör. \"yayılanın yarısı kadar yükselt\") hatırlıyorum ama **bu sayıyı\n  yazmıyorum — kaynaksız sayı yazmak bu kartta yasak.**\n\n## 5. Endüstri nasıl doğruluyor + tolerans + hangi çizgi — **DOĞRULANMADI**\n\n- **\"Walking the pattern\"** (kalıbı yürütmek): iki parçayı dikiş çizgisi boyunca\n  adım adım üst üste çevirerek eşleştirme. Elle yapılan geleneksel yöntem. →\n  **DOĞRULANMADI** (kaynak çekilemedi).\n- **CLO3D `Check Sewing Length`** — dikiş nesnesinin iki tarafının uzunluk farkını\n  eşik üstünde renklendirir. Eşiğin **1 mm** olduğu bu repoda başka yerde yazılı;\n  **ben bu turda CLO3D dokümanını AÇAMADIM**, o yüzden bağımsız doğrulamam yok.\n  → **DOĞRULANMADI.**\n- **Gerber AccuMark `Walk Pieces`** — aynı işin yazılımdaki karşılığı. →\n  **DOĞRULANMADI.**\n- **Kabul edilen ölçüm toleransı:** elle çalışmada **1/8\" (3.2 mm)** sık anılır;\n  yazılımda **1 mm** mertebesi. İkisi de **kaynaksız**, kapıya konamaz.\n- **Kesim çizgisinde mi, dikiş çizgisinde mi?** Doğru cevabın \"**dikiş\n  çizgisinde**\" olduğunu düşünüyorum (yürütme dikiş çizgisi boyunca yapılır,\n  yazılımda da dikiş nesnesi dikiş çizgisine oturur), ama **doğrulayamadım.**\n  Bu, işaretin döndüğü yer olduğu için (içbükey oyuk ofsette uzar, dışbükey kapak\n  kısalır) kartın en pahalı sorusu ve **cevapsız kaldı.**\n\n---\n\n## KONU DIŞI AMA ÖNEMLİ (bu turda görülen)\n\n1. **Gece koşusunun bu kolu boşa gitti.** Araştırma ajanına web izni verilmeden\n   araştırma kartı açılmış. Sonraki koşuda kart açılmadan önce `WebSearch` /\n   `WebFetch` izninin ajan tanımında açık olduğu kontrol edilmeli; yoksa kart\n   hiç açılmamalı.\n2. **Bu kartın çıktısı bir kapıya besleniyordu.** Kapı bu defterle\n   tasarlanamaz. Bir sonraki işçi eşik beklerse **boş dönecek** — o yüzden §KAPI\n   bölümü aşağıda \"eşik yok\" diye AÇIKÇA yazıldı, uydurma bir sayıyla\n   doldurulmadı.\n3. **Kartın 5. sorusu (hangi çizgi) aslında bir ölçüm sorusu, araştırma sorusu\n   değil.** Kesim/dikiş çizgisi ayrımı geometriyle ölçülebilir; hangi çizgide\n   ölçüldüğünde işaretin döndüğü hesapla çıkar. Araştırmadan beklenen tek şey\n   \"endüstri hangisini kullanıyor\" konvansiyonu. Kart bu ikisini karıştırıyor.\n\n## SONRAKİ İŞÇİ İÇİN ARAMA LİSTESİ (web izni açıldığında)\nSırayla, en sertten en yumuşağa:\n1. Aldrich, *Metric Pattern Cutting for Women's Wear* — sleeve head / cap ease\n   tablosu (birincil kitap; Google Books veya kütüphane taraması).\n2. Armstrong, *Patternmaking for Fashion Design* — sleeve cap ease bölümü.\n3. Müller & Sohn (Rundschau) — Ärmelkugel Mehrweite tabloları (metrik, tablolu).\n4. CLO3D resmi manual → `Check Sewing Length` eşik değeri ve hangi çizgide.\n5. Gerber/Lectra AccuMark manual → `Walk Pieces`.\n6. ASTM D5585 / ASTM tekstil standartları — beden tablosu mu tolerans mı olduğu\n   netleştirilmeli (muhtemelen tolerans DEĞİL, sadece ölçü tablosu).\n7. Threads Magazine cap-ease yazıları (ikincil, sadece bant genişliği için).\n\n---\n\n## KAPI İÇİN ÖNERİLEN EŞİK\n\n**EŞİK ÖNERİLMİYOR. dx * 0.55, from.y + dy * 0.98})};\n}\nstatic double capCurveLengthRep(double width, double capHeight) {\n    const Point left{-width / 2, capHeight};\n    const Point right{width / 2, capHeight};\n    const Point top{0, 0};\n    std::vector<PathCommand> cmds{PathCommand::move(left)};\n    for (const auto& c : capCurveRep(left, top, true)) cmds.push_back(c);\n    for (const auto& c : capCurveRep(top, right, false)) cmds.push_back(c);\n    return pathLength(cmds);\n}\n// ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nstruct SleeveSolve {\n    double bicepsEstimate = 0;\n    double step1Width = 0;      // width the length-match bisection returns\n    bool floorTriggered = false; // step 2 (biceps floor) overrode step 1\n    double finalWidth = 0;\n    double finalCapHeight = 0;  // BEFORE the puff rise\n    double targetCapLength = 0;\n};\n\nstatic SleeveSolve solveSleeve(const BodyMeasurementsSnapshot& m, double armholeLength,\n                               double armholeDepth, Fabric fabric) {\n    SleeveSolve s;\n    s.bicepsEstimate = m.bustMM() * SleeveBlock::bicepsRatio * (1 + SleeveBlock::bicepsEaseFor(fabric));\n    double capHeight = armholeDepth * 0.75;\n    s.targetCapLength = armholeLength * (1 + SleeveBlock::capEaseFor(fabric));\n    double lo = 60.0;\n    double hi = std::max(s.bicepsEstimate, 200.0) * 3;\n    while (capCurveLengthRep(hi, capHeight) < s.targetCapLength && hi < 8000) hi *= 1.5;\n    double width = std::max(s.bicepsEstimate, 200.0);\n    for (int i = 0; i < 60; ++i) {\n        width = (lo + hi) / 2;\n        const double delta = capCurveLengthRep(width, capHeight) - s.targetCapLength;\n        if (std::fabs(delta) <= SleeveBlock::convergenceTolerance) break;\n        if (delta > 0) hi = width; else lo = width;\n    }\n    s.step1Width = width;\n    if (width < s.bicepsEstimate) {\n        s.floorTriggered = true;\n        width = s.bicepsEstimate;\n        double chLo = 20.0, chHi = capHeight;\n        for (int i = 0; i < 60; ++i) {\n            capHeight = (chLo + chHi) / 2;\n            const double delta = capCurveLengthRep(width, capHeight) - s.targetCapLength;\n            if (std::fabs(delta) <= SleeveBlock::convergenceTolerance) break;\n            if (delta > 0) chHi = capHeight; else chLo = capHeight;\n        }\n    }\n    s.finalWidth = width;\n    s.finalCapHeight = capHeight;\n    return s;\n}\n\nstatic double edgeLength(const PatternPiece& p, size_t idx) {\n    if (idx == 0 || idx >= p.commands.size()) return -1;\n    // start point = end point of the previous command\n    const Point start = p.commands[idx - 1].to;\n    return pathLength({PathCommand::move(start), p.commands[idx]});\n}\n\n// Same three-command run as engine/tests/cap_sleeve_check.cpp:50.\nstatic double capEdgeLength(const PatternPiece& p) {\n    if (p.commands.size() < 3) return 0;\n    std::vector<PathCommand> cap{p.commands[0], p.commands[1], p.commands[2]};\n    return pathLength(cap);\n}\n\nstruct HalfDrawn { double len = 0; bool layoutOk = true; int pieces = 1; };\n\nstatic HalfDrawn drawnHalf(const PatternPiece& main, const PatternPiece* side, bool princess) {\n    HalfDrawn h;\n    if (!princess) {\n        h.layoutOk = main.commands.size() > 3 &&\n                     main.commands[2].type == CmdType::Line &&\n                     main.commands[3].type == CmdType::Curve;\n        h.len = edgeLength(main, 3);\n        return h;\n    }\n    h.pieces = 2;\n    h.layoutOk = main.commands.size() > 3 &&\n                 main.commands[2].type == CmdType::Line &&\n                 main.commands[3].type == CmdType::Curve &&\n                 side && side->commands.size() > 1 &&\n                 side->commands[0].type F6-B ölçtü: Gathered beyan 0.20 ↔ çizilen 0.1101–0.1390;\nPuffed beyan 0.45 ↔ çizilen 0.7164–0.7910. **Bu hüküm bugün DÜŞER; düşmesi\nkapının çalıştığının kanıtıdır.**\n\nKapı 8 bedeni (EU34..EU48) × {Woven, Knit} × {Plain, Gathered, Puffed} × pens\nve prenses modunu gezsin. 0.24 : 0.18;\n15\t    return {PathCommand::curve(\n16\t        to,\n17\t        {from.x + dx * hollow, from.y + dy * 0.02},\n18\t        {from.x + dx * 0.55, from.y + dy * 0.98})};\n19\t}\n20\t\n21\tdouble capCurveLength(double width, double capHeight) {\n22\t    const Point left{-width / 2, capHeight};\n23\t    const Point right{width / 2, capHeight};\n24\t    const Point top{0, 0};\n25\t    std::vector<PathCommand> cmds{PathCommand::move(left)};\n26\t    for (const auto& c : capCurve(left, top, true)) cmds.push_back(c);\n27\t    for (const auto& c : capCurve(top, right, false)) cmds.push_back(c);\n28\t    return pathLength(cmds);\n29\t}\n30\t\n31\tdouble totalLength(SleeveLength length, double armLengthMM, double capHeight) {\n32\t    switch (length) {\n33\t        case SleeveLength::Short: return capHeight + 90;\n34\t        case SleeveLength::Elbow: return capHeight + armLengthMM * 0.35;\n35\t        case SleeveLength::Long: return armLengthMM * 0.96;\n36\t    }\n37\t    return capHeight + 90;\n38\t}\n39\t\n40\t} // namespace\n41\t\n42\tstd::vector<PatternPiece> draft(\n43\t    const BodyMeasurementsSnapshot& m,\n44\t    SleeveStyle style,\n45\t    SleeveLength length,\n46\t    double armholeLength,\n47\t    double armholeDepth,\n48\t    Fabric fabric,\n49\t    SleeveCap cap\n50\t) {\n51\t    if (style == SleeveStyle::None) return {};\n52\t\n53\t    const double bicepsEstimate = m.bustMM() * bicepsRatio * (1 + bicepsEaseFor(fabric));\n54\t    double capHeight = armholeDepth * 0.75;\n55\t    const double targetCapLength = armholeLength * (1 + capEaseFor(fabric));\n56\t\n57\t    // Fit the cap to the armhole while keeping the sleeve wide enough for the\n58\t    // arm. The biceps line is the HARD floor: a sleeve narrower than the biceps\n59\t    // girth + ease binds or won't close at the underarm. Classic set-in drafting\n60\t    // (Aldrich/Brian) draws the biceps line first, then trues the cap to the\n61\t    // armhole by adjusting the cap HEIGHT — not the width.\n62\t    //\n63\t    // Step 1: solve width by length-matching at the default cap height. Cap\n64\t    // length grows monotonically with width, so bisect (a multiplicative walk\n65\t    // oscillates and can miss the tolerance).\n66\t    double lo = 60.0;\n67\t    double hi = std::max(bicepsEstimate, 200.0) * 3;\n68\t    while (capCurveLength(hi, capHeight) < targetCapLength && hi < 8000) {\n69\t        hi *= 1.5;\n70\t    }\n71\t    double width = std::max(bicepsEstimate, 200.0);\n72\t    for (int i = 0; i < 60; ++i) {\n73\t        width = (lo + hi) / 2;\n74\t        const double delta = capCurveLength(width, capHeight) - targetCapLength;\n75\t        if (std::fabs(delta) <= convergenceTolerance) break;\n76\t        if (delta > 0) hi = width; else lo = width;\n77\t    }\n78\t\n79\t    // Step 2: if that width is below the biceps floor (the common case — a\n80\t    // length-matched cap on the default height comes out too narrow for the\n81\t    // arm), widen to the biceps and instead lower the cap height until the cap\n82\t    // length matches again. A wider sleeve on a shallower cap is the correct,\n83\t    // sewable relaxed-set trade; the cap ease stays inside the validator window.\n84\t    if (width < bicepsEstimate) {\n85\t        width = bicepsEstimate;\n86\t        double chLo = 20.0, chHi = capHeight; // shallower cap => shorter cap length\n87\t        // At width=biceps the default height overshoots the target, so the\n88\t        // answer sits below capHeight; bisect the height down to the target.\n89\t        for (int i = 0; i < 60; ++i) {\n90\t            capHeight = (chLo + chHi) / 2;\n91\t            const double delta = capCurveLength(width, capHeight) - targetCapLength;\n92\t            if (std::fabs(delta) <= convergenceTolerance) break;\n93\t            if (delta > 0) chHi = capHeight; else chLo = capHeight;\n94\t        }\n95\t    }\n96\t\n97\t    // GATHERED / PUFF HEAD (Loop 6). Slash-and-spread across the crown: the cap\n98\t    // is widened by `spread` and the top point is drawn HIGHER so the extra arc\n99\t    // is gathered/eased into the SAME armhole (crown gather between the notches;\n100\t    // the underarm-to-notch length still matches the armhole 1:1). VERIFIED rule\n101\t    // (dresspatternmaking / M.Müller): a puff RAISES the cap by the spread amount;\n102\t    // a plain gathered head keeps the height. The biceps/hem width below is left\n103\t    // on the fitted base `width` so the sleeve still clears the arm. See FORMULAS.\n104\t    const double spread = capSpreadFrac(cap) * width;      // 0 when Plain / Cap\n105\t    const double capWidth = width + spread;                // widened crown\n106\t    const double capRise = (cap == SleeveCap::Puffed) ? spread : 0.0;\n107\t    capHeight += capRise;                                  // Plain/Cap: +0 → identical\n108\t\n109\t    // CAP SLEEVE WING (R1.2). A cap sleeve keeps the ordinary set-in cap head\n110\t    // (the same cap curve, fitted to the armhole above so it sets in 1:1) but has\n111\t    // NO underarm seam and NO length — the outer edge sweeps a short depth below\n112\t    // the crown and back up to the underarm points, so a small wing covers the\n113\t    // top of the shoulder and dies at the underarm. We draw it here as its own\n114\t    // outline and return early; the full-sleeve body below is skipped. Only a\n115\t    // Short cap is a cap sleeve — length is ignored (a cap has no length axis).\n116\t    if (cap == SleeveCap::Cap) {\n117\t        const double cHalf = width / 2;         // the fitted std::vector<PathCommand> capCmds{PathCommand::move(cl)};\n126\t        // cap head (armhole edge): left underarm → crown → right underarm, the\n127\t        // SAME S-curve the plain cap uses, so it
